[Operational Implications](#9-operational-implications)\n10. [Important Files and Directories](#10-important-files-and-directories)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Subsystem Purpose and Boundaries\n\nThe `k8s.io/apiserver` library (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/`) is a reusable framework for building production-grade, Kubernetes-style API servers. It is the foundation for `kube-apiserver` itself and for extension API servers written by third parties (aggregated API servers, CRD-backed servers, etc.).\n\n**What it does:**\n- Provides the `GenericAPIServer` runtime: HTTP serving, TLS, lifecycle management, health endpoints.\n- Implements the full request pipeline: authentication → authorization → priority-and-fairness → audit → admission → REST dispatch → storage.\n- Offers pluggable backends for every stage of that pipeline via well-defined Go interfaces.\n- Bridges between the HTTP world and the typed Go world via serialization, conversion, and versioning machinery sourced from `k8s.io/apimachinery`.\n\n**What it does not do:**\n- Define concrete API types (those live in `k8s.io/api` and in individual API group packages).\n- Run controllers or reconciliation loops (that is `controller-manager` territory).\n- Talk to clients other than etcd (outbound calls to webhooks and extension servers go through `k8s.io/client-go`).\n\n**Upstream / downstream dependencies:**\n\n| Direction | Dependency | Role |\n|-----------|-----------|------|\n| Upstream | `k8s.io/apimachinery` | Scheme, runtime.Object, serialization codecs, GVK primitives |\n| Upstream | `k8s.io/client-go` | REST client, informer factory (used by admission webhooks and PAF) |\n| Upstream | `etcd` (via `go.etcd.io/etcd/client/v3`) | Primary and only supported backing store |\n| Downstream | `kube-apiserver` (`cmd/kube-apiserver`) | Main consumer; calls `CreateServerChain` |\n| Downstream | `k8s.io/kube-aggregator` | Aggregation layer; embeds `GenericAPIServer` |\n| Downstream | `k8s.io/apiextensions-apiserver` | CRD server; embeds `GenericAPIServer` |\n| Downstream | Any aggregated API server | Extension servers built using `RecommendedOptions` |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 `pkg/server` — The Core Runtime\n\n**`Config` struct** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/config.go:112`)\nThe central configuration object for `GenericAPIServer`. It is populated from command-line flags via `Options` structs and passed to `New()`. Fields include `Authentication`, `Authorization`, `AdmissionControl`, `FlowControl`, `AuditBackend`, `BuildHandlerChainFunc`, and `SecureServing`. Notably, it exposes `BuildHandlerChainFunc` as a first-class escape hatch for replacing the entire filter chain.\n\n**`GenericAPIServer` struct** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:110`)\nThe server instance. It holds the `APIServerHandler` (which carries the live `http.Handler`), its delegation target, lifecycle signals, post-start hooks, pre-shutdown hooks, health registries, and the audit backend. The type does not export a `Start()` method directly; callers must first call `PrepareRun()` which returns the opaque `preparedGenericAPIServer`, and then call `RunWithContext()` on that.\n\n**`APIServerHandler` struct** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/handler.go:42`)\nHolds three layered handlers:\n- `FullHandlerChain` — the entire filter-decorated handler. `ServeHTTP` on this struct dispatches here.\n- `GoRestfulContainer` — the `go-restful` WebService container used for registered API groups.\n- `NonGoRestfulMux` — a `PathRecorderMux` for everything else (healthz, metrics, openapi, debug).\n- `Director` — chooses between the two above based on registered path prefixes.\n\n**`DelegationTarget` interface** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:312`)\nThe delegation chain contract. Each server in the chain implements this; unhandled requests fall through to `NextDelegate()`. `emptyDelegate` terminates the chain, returning 404.\n\n**`lifecycleSignals`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/lifecycle_signals.go:112`)\nNamed channels signaling state transitions: `ShutdownInitiated`, `AfterShutdownDelayDuration`, `PreShutdownHooksStopped`, `NotAcceptingNewRequest`, `InFlightRequestsDrained`, `HTTPServerStoppedListening`, `HasBeenReady`, `MuxAndDiscoveryComplete`. Filters in the handler chain subscribe to these channels to drain requests gracefully during shutdown.\n\n### 2.2 `pkg/endpoints` — HTTP-to-REST Bridge\n\n**`APIInstaller`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/installer.go:62`)\nIntrospects a `rest.Storage` implementation at server startup and registers the appropriate go-restful routes for each supported verb (GET, LIST, WATCH, CREATE, UPDATE, PATCH, DELETE, and subresources). Routes are annotated with `x-kubernetes-action` and `x-kubernetes-group-version-kind` metadata used by OpenAPI generation.\n\n**Verb handlers** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/handlers/`)\nConcrete handlers for each HTTP method: `create.go`, `update.go`, `delete.go`, `get.go`, `patch.go`, `watch.go`. Each reads the request body, negotiates content type, calls into the admission chain, and dispatches to the underlying `rest.Storage`. For example, `createHandler` (`handlers/create.go:53`) deserializes the body only after the filter chain has authenticated and authorized the request.\n\n**`RequestScope`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/handlers/`)\nA per-route context snapshot containing the serializer, kind, namer, and admission interface — passed to every verb handler at dispatch time, eliminating repeated lookups.\n\n### 2.3 `pkg/admission` — Policy Enforcement\n\n**`Interface` / `MutationInterface` / `ValidationInterface`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/interfaces.go:122`)\nThe admission plugin contracts. `MutationInterface.Admit` may modify the object; `ValidationInterface.Validate` may only reject it. Both receive `Attributes` (operation, object, old object, user info) and `ObjectInterfaces` (convertor, defaulter).\n\n**`chainAdmissionHandler`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/chain.go:23`)\nA `[]Interface` that implements both mutation and validation by iterating plugins in order and short-circuiting on the first error.\n\n**`reinvoker`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/reinvocation.go`)\nWraps the mutation chain to re-run mutating plugins after any individual plugin sets `ShouldReinvoke()` on the `ReinvocationContext`. This supports webhook plugins whose mutations may invalidate earlier plugins' assumptions.\n\n**Built-in admission plugins** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/plugin/`)\nThe library ships with: `namespace/lifecycle`, `resourcequota`, `webhook/mutating`, `webhook/validating`, `policy/mutating` (MutatingAdmissionPolicy, alpha), `policy/validating` (ValidatingAdmissionPolicy, GA).\n\n### 2.4 `pkg/storage` — Storage Abstraction\n\n**`storage.Interface`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/storage/interfaces.go`)\nThe storage contract defining `Create`, `Get`, `GuaranteedUpdate`, `Delete`, `Watch`, and `List`. The only production implementation today is `etcd3`.\n\n**`Cacher`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/storage/cacher/cacher.go:262`)\nAn in-memory write-through layer placed in front of the raw etcd client. It maintains a `watchCache` (a sliding-window event ring buffer) and a `cache.Reflector`. WATCH and LIST requests are primarily served from memory; only consistent reads must wait for (or fetch directly from) etcd.\n\n**`genericregistry.Store`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/registry/generic/registry/store.go:100`)\nThe standard CRUD implementation used by built-in and extension resources. It wraps `storage.Interface` in a `DryRunnableStorage` wrapper (to handle dry-run requests) and calls into resource-specific `Strategy` objects for validation, defaulting, and conflict detection.\n\n### 2.5 `pkg/util/flowcontrol` — Priority and Fairness\n\n**`apf_controller`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/apf_controller.go`)\nA controller that watches `FlowSchema` and `PriorityLevelConfiguration` objects and configures a set of `QueueSet` fair queues. When a request arrives, the controller matches it against `FlowSchema` rules (by user, group, verb, resource, namespace) and assigns it to a priority level.\n\n**`QueueSet` / fair queuing** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/`)\nImplements shuffle-sharding fair queuing. Each priority level has multiple queues. A request's queue is selected by hashing its flow distinguisher (typically `<username>/<namespace>`) against the queue count (shuffle sharding). Work is measured in seat-seconds via a `WorkEstimator`; list requests with many objects cost more \"seats\" than simple GETs.\n\n### 2.6 `pkg/audit` — Audit Logging\n\n**`Sink` / `Backend`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/types.go:22`)\n`Sink.ProcessEvents` receives audit events at each stage of a request. `Backend` extends `Sink` with `Run/Shutdown` lifecycle. Multiple backends may be unioned with `audit.Union`.\n\n**`PolicyRuleEvaluator`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/evaluator.go:40`)\nEvaluates the audit policy file against authorizer attributes and returns a `RequestAuditConfig` with a `Level` (None/Metadata/Request/RequestResponse) and stages to omit.\n\n---\n\n## 3. End-to-End Request Flow\n\nThe following describes what happens from the time an HTTP request arrives until a response is sent. File citations reference the actual construction order in `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/config.go:1028`).\n\n> **Key insight**: `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` builds the chain from the _inside out_ — the innermost handler (closest to storage) is wrapped by successively outer filters. The code reads bottom-to-top in the source; the first handler to receive a request is the outermost one (last applied in the source).\n\n### 3.1 Filter Chain (outermost to innermost)\n\n```\nClient\n  │\n  ▼\n[1] WithAuditInit               — allocates an AuditContext into the request context\n[2] WithPanicRecovery           — recovers from panics; logs stack trace; returns 500\n[3] WithMuxAndDiscoveryComplete — returns 503 if the server has not finished installing paths\n[4] WithRequestReceivedTimestamp— stamps arrival time into context\n[5] WithRequestInfo             — parses URL into RequestInfo (verb, resource, namespace, name)\n[6] WithRoutine (optional)      — offloads request to a separate goroutine (APIServingWithRoutine gate)\n[7] WithHTTPLogging             — structured HTTP access log\n[8] WithRetryAfter (optional)   — during shutdown, 429 + Retry-After header\n[9] WithHSTS                    — adds Strict-Transport-Security header\n[10] WithCacheControl           — sets Cache-Control: no-cache\n[11] WithProbabilisticGoaway    — randomly sends HTTP/2 GOAWAY to load-balance long-running connections\n[12] WithWatchTermination...    — drains watch requests during shutdown\n[13] WithWaitGroup              — adds non-long-running requests to a SafeWaitGroup for graceful drain\n[14] WithRequestDeadline        — enforces overall request deadline from context\n[15] WithTimeoutForNonLong...   — runs handler in a goroutine with a deadline; returns 504 on timeout\n[16] WithWarningRecorder        — installs a warning header writer (thread-safe under the timeout wrapper)\n[17] WithCORS                   — preflight and Access-Control-* headers\n[18] WithAuthentication         — calls Authenticator chain; injects UserInfo into context; 401 on failure\n[19] WithTracing (optional)     — creates OpenTelemetry span from authenticated context\n[20] WithAudit                  — emits audit events at RequestReceived and ResponseComplete stages\n[21] WithImpersonation          — applies impersonation headers (if Impersonate-* headers are present)\n[22] WithPriorityAndFairness /  — classifies request into a FlowSchema/PriorityLevel; queues or rejects\n    WithMaxInFlightLimit\n[23] WithAuthorization          — calls Authorizer chain; 403 on denial\n[24] (API handler / REST dispatch)\n      │\n      ▼\n    Director → GoRestfulContainer → APIInstaller route\n                                        │\n                                        ▼\n                                  Verb handler (e.g. createHandler)\n                                        │\n                                        ├── Deserialize request body\n                                        ├── Default object fields\n                                        ├── Admission: Mutate (Admit)\n                                        ├── Validate (Strategy + admission Validate)\n                                        └── genericregistry.Store.Create/Update/Delete\n                                                │\n                                                ▼\n                                          DryRunnableStorage\n                                                │\n                                                ▼\n                                          Cacher / etcd3\n```\n\n### 3.2 A Concrete CREATE Example\n\n1. **Request arrives**: `POST /apis/apps/v1/namespaces/default/deployments`\n2. `WithRequestInfo` parses this as `{verb:create, resource:deployments, group:apps, version:v1, namespace:default}`.\n3. `WithAuthentication` validates the bearer token against the OIDC/x509 authenticator chain. UserInfo is injected.\n4. `WithPriorityAndFairness` matches the request to a `FlowSchema` (e.g. `system:authenticated`), places it in a queue for the `workload-high` priority level. The `WorkEstimator` assigns 1 seat (simple mutating write).\n5. `WithAuthorization` calls RBAC — checks if the user has `create` on `deployments` in `default` namespace.\n6. `WithAudit` emits a `RequestReceived` event.\n7. The go-restful `Director` identifies the path is registered under `/apis/apps/v1` and dispatches to the registered go-restful route.\n8. `createHandler` (`handlers/create.go:53`) is invoked:\n   - Content negotiation for request and response encodings.\n   - `limitedReadBodyWithRecordMetric` reads the body (up to `MaxRequestBodyBytes`).\n   - Decoder deserializes body into internal type (`apps/v1` → internal `apps`).\n   - Defaulting functions set optional fields.\n   - Admission `Admit` phase: mutating webhook plugins (and `MutatingAdmissionPolicy`) are called.\n   - Admission `Validate` phase: validating admission plugins (Strategy + `ValidatingAdmissionWebhook` + `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy`) are called.\n   - `genericregistry.Store.Create` is invoked → `DryRunnableStorage.Create` → `Cacher.Create` → `etcd3.Create`.\n   - `WithAudit` emits `ResponseComplete` with the response object (if audit level ≥ `RequestResponse`).\n9. HTTP 201 Created is written back to the client.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Server Composition and Delegation Chain\n\n`kube-apiserver` is not a single server; it is a chain of three `GenericAPIServer` instances assembled in `cmd/kube-apiserver/app/server.go:CreateServerChain`.\n\n```\nIncoming request\n      │\n      ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  Aggregator Server          │  (kube-aggregator)\n│  Handles: APIService CRD    │\n│  Proxies: extension servers │\n└──────────┬──────────────────┘\n           │ delegate (not an APIService path)\n           ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  Kube API Server (core)     │\n│  Handles: /api/v1, /apis/*  │\n│  built-in groups             │\n└──────────┬──────────────────┘\n           │ delegate (unknown path)\n           ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  API Extensions Server      │  (apiextensions-apiserver)\n│  Handles: CRDs dynamically  │\n└──────────┬──────────────────┘\n           │ delegate (end of chain)\n           ▼\n      emptyDelegate → 404\n```\n\n**How delegation works**: Each `GenericAPIServer` stores a `delegationTarget DelegationTarget`. When the `Director`'s `NonGoRestfulMux` cannot match a path, it calls the `UnprotectedHandler()` of the delegation target — effectively bypassing the *target's* filter chain (authentication, authorization, etc. run only once, in the outermost server). This is why `UnprotectedHandler()` returns the target's `Handler.Director` directly, not its `FullHandlerChain`.\n\n**Post-start and pre-shutdown hooks** are aggregated across the chain: each server's hooks are merged with its delegation target's hooks so that a single `RunWithContext` drives the entire system.\n\n---\n\n## 5. API Group Registration\n\n### 5.1 The `runtime.Scheme`\n\nThe `runtime.Scheme` is the type registry for a group. It provides:\n- **GVK ↔ Go type mapping**: registers types so the serialization framework can create the right Go struct from a JSON/protobuf payload.\n- **Conversion functions**: the hub-and-spoke model converts all versions through a single internal type (the \"hub\"). `conversion-gen` generates most conversion functions; handwritten conversions handle non-trivial field renames.\n- **Defaulting functions**: `defaulter-gen` generates `SetDefaults_*` functions that are registered into the scheme and called during decode.\n- **Deep-copy functions**: `deepcopy-gen` generates `DeepCopyObject()` required by `runtime.Object`. This is safety-critical: objects returned from the watch cache must be deep-copied before modification to prevent data races.\n\n### 5.2 `APIGroupInfo` and Registration\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:72\ntype APIGroupInfo struct {\n    PrioritizedVersions         []schema.GroupVersion\n    VersionedResourcesStorageMap map[string]map[string]rest.Storage\n    Scheme                      *runtime.Scheme\n    NegotiatedSerializer        runtime.NegotiatedSerializer\n    ParameterCodec              runtime.ParameterCodec\n}\n```\n\n`VersionedResourcesStorageMap` maps `version → resource-name → rest.Storage`. Each resource can have multiple sub-resources (e.g., `\"deployments/status\"`, `\"deployments/scale\"`), each with its own `rest.Storage` implementation.\n\n`GenericAPIServer.InstallAPIGroups` calls `APIInstaller.Install()` which:\n1. Iterates resources in `VersionedResourcesStorageMap`.\n2. For each resource, introspects which `rest.*` interfaces its `Storage` implements (`rest.Getter`, `rest.Lister`, `rest.Creater`, `rest.Updater`, `rest.GracefulDeleter`, `rest.Watcher`, etc.) via type assertion.\n3. Registers go-restful routes for only the verbs supported by that storage.\n4. Populates discovery documents and OpenAPI schema at the same time.\n\n### 5.3 The `Strategy` Pattern\n\nEach resource's `genericregistry.Store` is configured with up to three strategy objects:\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/registry/generic/registry/store.go:172\nCreateStrategy rest.RESTCreateStrategy\nUpdateStrategy rest.RESTUpdateStrategy\nDeleteStrategy rest.RESTDeleteStrategy\n```\n\nA strategy object implements the resource-specific validation and business logic that is shared across all storage backends. For example, `Deployment`'s strategy validates pod template specs, resets fields that users may not set (like `status`), and enforces name generation rules. This separation keeps the generic storage machinery clean and resource-specific logic co-located with its type definition.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Storage Layer and Watch Cache\n\n### 6.1 Architecture\n\n```\nREST handler\n    │\n    ▼\ngenericregistry.Store\n    │  (wraps in DryRunnableStorage for dry-run)\n    ▼\nCacher  (staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/storage/cacher/cacher.go:262)\n    │   ┌──────────────────────────┐\n    │   │  watchCache              │  ← ring buffer of recent events\n    │   │  cache.Reflector         │  ← LIST + WATCH from etcd\n    │   └──────────────────────────┘\n    │  (falls through for consistent reads or cache misses)\n    ▼\netcd3.Store (grpc to etcd cluster)\n```\n\n### 6.2 Cacher Internals\n\nThe `Cacher` struct (`cacher.go:262`) maintains:\n- `watchCache`: a fixed-capacity ring buffer of `watchCacheEvent` objects, protected by an `RWMutex`. The buffer size defaults to 100 (`EtcdOptions.DefaultWatchCacheSize`), configurable per-resource via `--watch-cache-sizes`.\n- `reflector`: a `cache.Reflector` that issues a single LIST + ongoing WATCH against etcd for the resource prefix. Events are pushed into `incoming chan watchCacheEvent` and then dispatched by the cacher's main goroutine to registered `cacheWatcher` clients.\n- `watchers`: an `indexedWatchers` map that organizes active client watches by namespace and by an optional indexed trigger (e.g., for pod watches filtered by `spec.nodeName`). The trigger optimization avoids waking every watcher on every event.\n\n### 6.3 Serving Semantics\n\n| Request type | Served from |\n|---|---|\n| `?watch=true&resourceVersion=0` | In-memory ring buffer from the start |\n| `?watch=true&resourceVersion=<rv>` | In-memory buffer if rv is within window; otherwise etcd watch |\n| `LIST` (no RV, or `resourceVersion=0`) | Watch cache store snapshot |\n| Consistent `LIST` (`resourceVersionMatch=NotOlderThan` with progress notify) | Waits for cache to advance past etcd's latest rev; served from cache |\n| `LIST` with exact `resourceVersion` and a limit | Falls through to etcd (pagination not fully cached) |\n\nBookmarks (empty `WATCH` events carrying only a `resourceVersion`) prevent the cache's RV from becoming stale — a stale cache would force expensive relist operations on clients.\n\n### 6.4 Optimistic Concurrency\n\nEvery object stored in etcd carries a `resourceVersion` that maps 1-to-1 to etcd's global `mod_revision`. On `UPDATE` or `PATCH`, `GuaranteedUpdate` performs a `txn` in etcd that only commits if the key's `mod_revision` matches the expected value. A mismatch returns `409 Conflict`, requiring the client to re-read and resubmit. This is pure optimistic locking — no server-side distributed locks are held during request processing.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Extension Points and Customization Hooks\n\n### 7.1 Custom Handler Chain (`BuildHandlerChainFunc`)\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/config.go:186\nBuildHandlerChainFunc func(apiHandler http.Handler, c *Config) (secure http.Handler)\n```\n\nBy replacing `Config.BuildHandlerChainFunc`, a consumer can insert, remove, or reorder any filter in the chain. The library ships two implementations:\n- `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` — the standard production chain.\n- `BuildHandlerChainWithStorageVersionPrecondition` — adds `WithStorageVersionPrecondition` (used by `kube-apiserver`) to gate requests until storage version migration is complete.\n\n**Risk**: Replacing the handler chain can silently disable authentication or authorization. Any custom chain should call `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` and wrap its result, rather than starting from scratch.\n\n### 7.2 Post-Start and Pre-Shutdown Hooks\n\n```go\n// GenericAPIServer method\nAddPostStartHook(name string, hook PostStartHookFunc) error\nAddPreShutdownHook(name string, hook preShutdownHookFunc) error\n```\n\nPost-start hooks run after the server starts listening, in separate goroutines. They receive a `StopCh` channel that closes when shutdown is initiated. Pre-shutdown hooks run before the HTTP server stops accepting connections — they are appropriate for draining outbound connections or flipping health check states.\n\n**Risk**: Slow or blocking post-start hooks delay the server's `HasBeenReady` signal, causing the process to be killed by liveness probes in managed environments.\n\n### 7.3 Admission Plugins\n\nAdmission plugins are the primary policy enforcement extension point. A custom in-process plugin must:\n\n1. Implement `admission.Interface` (and optionally `MutationInterface` or `ValidationInterface`).\n2. Register itself: `plugins.Register(PluginName, factory)` — where `plugins` is the `Plugins` registry passed into `AdmissionOptions`.\n3. If it needs shared resources (e.g., an informer factory or a Kubernetes client), implement the appropriate `WantsX` interface from `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/initializer/interfaces.go`:\n   - `WantsExternalKubeInformerFactory`\n   - `WantsAuthorizer`\n   - `WantsRESTMapper`\n   - `WantsFeatures`\n\nFor external validation without in-process code, `ValidatingAdmissionWebhook` (`plugin/webhook/validating`) and the CEL-based `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy` (`plugin/policy/validating`) ship with the library and require no code changes.\n\n**Risk of re-invocation**: Mutating webhooks may be re-invoked if a sibling webhook set `ShouldReinvoke()`. Plugins must therefore be idempotent — applying the same mutation twice must have the same effect as applying it once.\n\n### 7.4 Authenticator and Authorizer Composition\n\nAuthentication is composed via `authenticatorunion` (first-success wins). Authorizers are composed via a chain where each authorizer can return Allow, Deny, or NoOpinion; NoOpinion passes to the next. Adding a custom authenticator or authorizer requires modifying `Config.Authentication.Authenticator` or `Config.Authorization.Authorizer` before `New()` is called.\n\nFor extension servers, `DelegatingAuthenticationOptions` and `DelegatingAuthorizationOptions` (in `pkg/server/options/`) issue `SubjectAccessReview` requests to the main `kube-apiserver`, delegating all auth decisions — this is the standard pattern for aggregated API servers.\n\n### 7.5 `RecommendedOptions` for Extension Servers\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/options/recommended.go:34\ntype RecommendedOptions struct {\n    Etcd           *EtcdOptions\n    SecureServing  *SecureServingOptionsWithLoopback\n    Authentication *DelegatingAuthenticationOptions\n    Authorization  *DelegatingAuthorizationOptions\n    Audit          *AuditOptions\n    Features       *FeatureOptions\n    CoreAPI        *CoreAPIOptions\n    Admission      *AdmissionOptions\n    EgressSelector *EgressSelectorOptions\n    Traces         *TracingOptions\n}\n```\n\nThis struct is the standard entry point for building a new aggregated API server. Calling `ApplyTo(config)` applies each option group to the server config in dependency order (etcd → egress → TLS → auth → audit → admission). Extension server authors only need to set the fields that differ from defaults.\n\n### 7.6 `genericregistry.Store` Hooks\n\nThe `Store` exposes three sets of lifecycle hooks per operation:\n\n| Hook | Timing | Use case |\n|------|--------|----------|\n| `BeginCreate` / `BeginUpdate` | Before storage write; returns commit/revert | Acquire side-effect resources transactionally |\n| `AfterCreate` / `AfterUpdate` / `AfterDelete` | After storage write, before decoration | Emit metrics, invalidate caches |\n| `Decorator` | On every object returned from storage | Inject computed fields not stored in etcd |\n| `ShouldDeleteDuringUpdate` | During update | Implement finalizer-style soft-delete |\n\n**Risk**: `BeginCreate/BeginUpdate` revert functions must be idempotent because they may be called more than once if the framework retries internally.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 8.1 etcd Dependency\n\nThe `apiserver` library has a hard dependency on a live etcd cluster. There is no in-memory fallback for production use. A full etcd partition causes write operations to fail immediately (etcd requires a quorum write). Read operations that hit the watch cache can continue to succeed for cached data, but consistent reads (`resourceVersionMatch=NotOlderThan`) will block until `waitUntilFreshAndBlock` times out.\n\n### 8.2 Watch Cache Eviction\n\nThe watch cache ring buffer is bounded. When a slow client falls behind the write rate, its cached RV drops out of the buffer window and the watcher receives an `ERROR` event with `410 Gone`. The client must restart its watch from `resourceVersion=0`. This is a known operational cliff: high write rates or large ring buffer sizes (configured per-resource) directly trade memory for client resilience. The default buffer size is 100 events per resource type.\n\n### 8.3 Priority and Fairness vs. MaxInFlight\n\nThe library supports two concurrency-control mechanisms:\n- **`MaxInFlightLimit`** (`pkg/server/filters/maxinflight.go`): a simple counting semaphore with separate limits for mutating (`MaxMutatingRequestsInFlight`) and non-mutating (`MaxRequestsInFlight`) requests. Any request exceeding the limit gets an immediate `429 Too Many Requests`.\n- **Priority and Fairness (APF)**: replaces `MaxInFlightLimit` when `Config.FlowControl` is non-nil. APF queues requests rather than immediately rejecting them, and enforces per-flow fairness using shuffle-sharded fair queuing.\n\nAPF is the default in `kube-apiserver` since 1.29. Extension servers using `RecommendedOptions` do not enable APF by default and fall back to `MaxInFlightLimit`. APF requires `FlowSchema` and `PriorityLevelConfiguration` objects in the API to exist before it can configure itself — creating a startup bootstrap dependency.\n\n### 8.4 Admission Re-invocation Overhead\n\nThe `reinvoker` wrapper (`pkg/admission/reinvocation.go`) can run the mutating admission chain twice per write request. With many heavy mutating webhooks, this doubles the worst-case webhook call latency. Operators should use `reinvocationPolicy: IfNeeded` selectively on `MutatingWebhookConfiguration` objects rather than globally.\n\n### 8.5 Handler Chain Construction Ordering\n\n`DefaultBuildHandlerChain` is built inside-out (code reads bottom-to-top). Misplacing a filter — e.g., moving `WithRequestInfo` after `WithAuthentication` — silently breaks features that depend on `RequestInfo` being in context (such as RBAC authorization, which inspects resource/verb). The framework does not validate the chain ordering at startup; bugs manifest at runtime under specific request patterns.\n\n### 8.6 Graceful Shutdown Sequencing\n\nThe shutdown flow (`RunWithContext`, `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:536`) relies on a precise signal ordering:\n\n```\nSIGTERM/ctx.Done()\n    → ShutdownInitiated (readyz → red)\n    → PreShutdownHooks run concurrently\n    → AfterShutdownDelayDuration (~70s default)\n    → NotAcceptingNewRequest\n        → NonLongRunningRequestWaitGroup.Wait (up to ShutdownTimeout ~60s)\n        → WatchRequestWaitGroup.Wait\n    → InFlightRequestsDrained\n        → HTTP server stops listening\n        → AuditBackend.Shutdown()\n```\n\nA pre-shutdown hook that blocks longer than `ShutdownDelayDuration` can prevent the server from draining active requests cleanly. `WithWaitGroup` ensures that each non-long-running request adds itself to `NonLongRunningRequestWaitGroup` only if it is not exempt from `RetryAfter` logic, so these two mechanisms must agree on what \"long-running\" means.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Operational Implications\n\n### 9.1 Performance: Watch Cache Sizing\n\nEach watched resource type maintains its own ring buffer. Resources with high write throughput (e.g., `Events`, `Pods` in large clusters) should have their cache size tuned via `--watch-cache-sizes=pods#1000,events#0`. Setting a resource's cache to 0 disables caching for that type and pushes all watch traffic directly to etcd — useful for resources that are rarely watched but have large objects.\n\nThe metric `watch_cache_resource_version` (label: `resource`) tracks the cache's current RV. A value significantly behind etcd's compaction watermark indicates the cache is not keeping up and clients will experience frequent relist events.\n\n### 9.2 Performance: Admission Latency\n\nEvery mutating or creating request passes through the full admission chain synchronously. Slow webhooks directly increase P99 latency for all writes. The filter `WithAudit` annotates audit events with per-stage timestamps; the metric `apiserver_admission_webhook_request_total{name, operation, type, rejected}` and `apiserver_admission_webhook_latencies_seconds` track per-webhook performance.\n\nTo isolate the impact: a single slow webhook adds its full RTT to every matching request. Webhook timeout defaults are 10s; a webhook timing out results in a 500 (fail-open) or a rejection (fail-closed), depending on `failurePolicy`.\n\n### 9.3 Reliability: Health Endpoints\n\nThe server exposes three health gates via `NonGoRestfulMux`:\n- `/healthz` — liveness. Fails only on catastrophic internal state. Added checks via `AddHealthChecks`.\n- `/readyz` — readiness. Fails during startup and the shutdown delay period (as soon as `ShutdownInitiated` fires). Custom storage readiness checks can be added via `StorageReadinessHook`.\n- `/livez` — liveness with a grace period (`livezGracePeriod`) that allows the server to start up without being killed by kubelet.\n\n`MuxAndDiscoveryComplete` is a lifecycle signal used by the `WithMuxAndDiscoveryComplete` filter to return `503` for requests that arrive before all paths are registered. This prevents 404 responses from being cached by clients during server startup.\n\n### 9.4 Maintainability: Code Generation Dependencies\n\nThe `apiserver` library's build correctness depends on running several code generators before compilation:\n- `deepcopy-gen`: for `DeepCopyObject()` on all registered types.\n- `conversion-gen`: for conversion functions between internal and external API versions.\n- `defaulter-gen`: for `SetDefaults_*` functions.\n- `openapi-gen`: for `GetOpenAPIDefinitions()` used to build the `/openapi/v2` and `/openapi/v3` specs.\n\nForgetting to regenerate code after adding a new field is a common source of subtle bugs — the field will be silently dropped during conversion if no conversion function copies it, and will be missing from OpenAPI if not annotated.\n\n### 9.5 Scalability: Resource Version Monotonicity\n\nAll `resourceVersion` values are etcd `mod_revision` values — globally monotonically increasing across all resources. This simplifies consistency reasoning (there is a total ordering of all writes) but means the RV of a `ConfigMap` and a `Pod` are comparable in magnitude. Clients that filter watches by a very high RV may cause the cacher's `waitUntilFreshAndBlock` to wait indefinitely if the RV is ahead of the cache — exposing a denial-of-service surface. The `WatchCacheReadWait` histogram tracks time spent in this blocking path.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Important Files and Directories\n\n```\nstaging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/\n├── ARCHITECTURE.md                          # High-level design overview (canonical)\n├── pkg/\n│   ├── server/\n│   │   ├── config.go                        # [Operational Implications](#9-operational-implications)\n10. [Important Files and Directories](#10-important-files-and-directories)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Subsystem Purpose and Boundaries\n\nThe `k8s.io/apiserver` library (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/`) is a reusable framework for building production-grade, Kubernetes-style API servers. It is the foundation for `kube-apiserver` itself and for extension API servers written by third parties (aggregated API servers, CRD-backed servers, etc.).\n\n**What it does:**\n- Provides the `GenericAPIServer` runtime: HTTP serving, TLS, lifecycle management, health endpoints.\n- Implements the full request pipeline: authentication → authorization → priority-and-fairness → audit → admission → REST dispatch → storage.\n- Offers pluggable backends for every stage of that pipeline via well-defined Go interfaces.\n- Bridges between the HTTP world and the typed Go world via serialization, conversion, and versioning machinery sourced from `k8s.io/apimachinery`.\n\n**What it does not do:**\n- Define concrete API types (those live in `k8s.io/api` and in individual API group packages).\n- Run controllers or reconciliation loops (that is `controller-manager` territory).\n- Talk to clients other than etcd (outbound calls to webhooks and extension servers go through `k8s.io/client-go`).\n\n**Upstream / downstream dependencies:**\n\n| Direction | Dependency | Role |\n|-----------|-----------|------|\n| Upstream | `k8s.io/apimachinery` | Scheme, runtime.Object, serialization codecs, GVK primitives |\n| Upstream | `k8s.io/client-go` | REST client, informer factory (used by admission webhooks and PAF) |\n| Upstream | `etcd` (via `go.etcd.io/etcd/client/v3`) | Primary and only supported backing store |\n| Downstream | `kube-apiserver` (`cmd/kube-apiserver`) | Main consumer; calls `CreateServerChain` |\n| Downstream | `k8s.io/kube-aggregator` | Aggregation layer; embeds `GenericAPIServer` |\n| Downstream | `k8s.io/apiextensions-apiserver` | CRD server; embeds `GenericAPIServer` |\n| Downstream | Any aggregated API server | Extension servers built using `RecommendedOptions` |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 `pkg/server` — The Core Runtime\n\n**`Config` struct** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/config.go:112`)\nThe central configuration object for `GenericAPIServer`. It is populated from command-line flags via `Options` structs and passed to `New()`. Fields include `Authentication`, `Authorization`, `AdmissionControl`, `FlowControl`, `AuditBackend`, `BuildHandlerChainFunc`, and `SecureServing`. Notably, it exposes `BuildHandlerChainFunc` as a first-class escape hatch for replacing the entire filter chain.\n\n**`GenericAPIServer` struct** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:110`)\nThe server instance. It holds the `APIServerHandler` (which carries the live `http.Handler`), its delegation target, lifecycle signals, post-start hooks, pre-shutdown hooks, health registries, and the audit backend. The type does not export a `Start()` method directly; callers must first call `PrepareRun()` which returns the opaque `preparedGenericAPIServer`, and then call `RunWithContext()` on that.\n\n**`APIServerHandler` struct** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/handler.go:42`)\nHolds three layered handlers:\n- `FullHandlerChain` — the entire filter-decorated handler. `ServeHTTP` on this struct dispatches here.\n- `GoRestfulContainer` — the `go-restful` WebService container used for registered API groups.\n- `NonGoRestfulMux` — a `PathRecorderMux` for everything else (healthz, metrics, openapi, debug).\n- `Director` — chooses between the two above based on registered path prefixes.\n\n**`DelegationTarget` interface** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:312`)\nThe delegation chain contract. Each server in the chain implements this; unhandled requests fall through to `NextDelegate()`. `emptyDelegate` terminates the chain, returning 404.\n\n**`lifecycleSignals`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/lifecycle_signals.go:112`)\nNamed channels signaling state transitions: `ShutdownInitiated`, `AfterShutdownDelayDuration`, `PreShutdownHooksStopped`, `NotAcceptingNewRequest`, `InFlightRequestsDrained`, `HTTPServerStoppedListening`, `HasBeenReady`, `MuxAndDiscoveryComplete`. Filters in the handler chain subscribe to these channels to drain requests gracefully during shutdown.\n\n### 2.2 `pkg/endpoints` — HTTP-to-REST Bridge\n\n**`APIInstaller`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/installer.go:62`)\nIntrospects a `rest.Storage` implementation at server startup and registers the appropriate go-restful routes for each supported verb (GET, LIST, WATCH, CREATE, UPDATE, PATCH, DELETE, and subresources). Routes are annotated with `x-kubernetes-action` and `x-kubernetes-group-version-kind` metadata used by OpenAPI generation.\n\n**Verb handlers** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/handlers/`)\nConcrete handlers for each HTTP method: `create.go`, `update.go`, `delete.go`, `get.go`, `patch.go`, `watch.go`. Each reads the request body, negotiates content type, calls into the admission chain, and dispatches to the underlying `rest.Storage`. For example, `createHandler` (`handlers/create.go:53`) deserializes the body only after the filter chain has authenticated and authorized the request.\n\n**`RequestScope`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/endpoints/handlers/`)\nA per-route context snapshot containing the serializer, kind, namer, and admission interface — passed to every verb handler at dispatch time, eliminating repeated lookups.\n\n### 2.3 `pkg/admission` — Policy Enforcement\n\n**`Interface` / `MutationInterface` / `ValidationInterface`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/interfaces.go:122`)\nThe admission plugin contracts. `MutationInterface.Admit` may modify the object; `ValidationInterface.Validate` may only reject it. Both receive `Attributes` (operation, object, old object, user info) and `ObjectInterfaces` (convertor, defaulter).\n\n**`chainAdmissionHandler`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/chain.go:23`)\nA `[]Interface` that implements both mutation and validation by iterating plugins in order and short-circuiting on the first error.\n\n**`reinvoker`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/reinvocation.go`)\nWraps the mutation chain to re-run mutating plugins after any individual plugin sets `ShouldReinvoke()` on the `ReinvocationContext`. This supports webhook plugins whose mutations may invalidate earlier plugins' assumptions.\n\n**Built-in admission plugins** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/plugin/`)\nThe library ships with: `namespace/lifecycle`, `resourcequota`, `webhook/mutating`, `webhook/validating`, `policy/mutating` (MutatingAdmissionPolicy, alpha), `policy/validating` (ValidatingAdmissionPolicy, GA).\n\n### 2.4 `pkg/storage` — Storage Abstraction\n\n**`storage.Interface`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/storage/interfaces.go`)\nThe storage contract defining `Create`, `Get`, `GuaranteedUpdate`, `Delete`, `Watch`, and `List`. The only production implementation today is `etcd3`.\n\n**`Cacher`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/storage/cacher/cacher.go:262`)\nAn in-memory write-through layer placed in front of the raw etcd client. It maintains a `watchCache` (a sliding-window event ring buffer) and a `cache.Reflector`. WATCH and LIST requests are primarily served from memory; only consistent reads must wait for (or fetch directly from) etcd.\n\n**`genericregistry.Store`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/registry/generic/registry/store.go:100`)\nThe standard CRUD implementation used by built-in and extension resources. It wraps `storage.Interface` in a `DryRunnableStorage` wrapper (to handle dry-run requests) and calls into resource-specific `Strategy` objects for validation, defaulting, and conflict detection.\n\n### 2.5 `pkg/util/flowcontrol` — Priority and Fairness\n\n**`apf_controller`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/apf_controller.go`)\nA controller that watches `FlowSchema` and `PriorityLevelConfiguration` objects and configures a set of `QueueSet` fair queues. When a request arrives, the controller matches it against `FlowSchema` rules (by user, group, verb, resource, namespace) and assigns it to a priority level.\n\n**`QueueSet` / fair queuing** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/util/flowcontrol/fairqueuing/queueset/`)\nImplements shuffle-sharding fair queuing. Each priority level has multiple queues. A request's queue is selected by hashing its flow distinguisher (typically `<username>/<namespace>`) against the queue count (shuffle sharding). Work is measured in seat-seconds via a `WorkEstimator`; list requests with many objects cost more \"seats\" than simple GETs.\n\n### 2.6 `pkg/audit` — Audit Logging\n\n**`Sink` / `Backend`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/types.go:22`)\n`Sink.ProcessEvents` receives audit events at each stage of a request. `Backend` extends `Sink` with `Run/Shutdown` lifecycle. Multiple backends may be unioned with `audit.Union`.\n\n**`PolicyRuleEvaluator`** (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/evaluator.go:40`)\nEvaluates the audit policy file against authorizer attributes and returns a `RequestAuditConfig` with a `Level` (None/Metadata/Request/RequestResponse) and stages to omit.\n\n---\n\n## 3. End-to-End Request Flow\n\nThe following describes what happens from the time an HTTP request arrives until a response is sent. File citations reference the actual construction order in `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` (`staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/config.go:1028`).\n\n> **Key insight**: `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` builds the chain from the _inside out_ — the innermost handler (closest to storage) is wrapped by successively outer filters. The code reads bottom-to-top in the source; the first handler to receive a request is the outermost one (last applied in the source).\n\n### 3.1 Filter Chain (outermost to innermost)\n\n```\nClient\n  │\n  ▼\n[1] WithAuditInit               — allocates an AuditContext into the request context\n[2] WithPanicRecovery           — recovers from panics; logs stack trace; returns 500\n[3] WithMuxAndDiscoveryComplete — returns 503 if the server has not finished installing paths\n[4] WithRequestReceivedTimestamp— stamps arrival time into context\n[5] WithRequestInfo             — parses URL into RequestInfo (verb, resource, namespace, name)\n[6] WithRoutine (optional)      — offloads request to a separate goroutine (APIServingWithRoutine gate)\n[7] WithHTTPLogging             — structured HTTP access log\n[8] WithRetryAfter (optional)   — during shutdown, 429 + Retry-After header\n[9] WithHSTS                    — adds Strict-Transport-Security header\n[10] WithCacheControl           — sets Cache-Control: no-cache\n[11] WithProbabilisticGoaway    — randomly sends HTTP/2 GOAWAY to load-balance long-running connections\n[12] WithWatchTermination...    — drains watch requests during shutdown\n[13] WithWaitGroup              — adds non-long-running requests to a SafeWaitGroup for graceful drain\n[14] WithRequestDeadline        — enforces overall request deadline from context\n[15] WithTimeoutForNonLong...   — runs handler in a goroutine with a deadline; returns 504 on timeout\n[16] WithWarningRecorder        — installs a warning header writer (thread-safe under the timeout wrapper)\n[17] WithCORS                   — preflight and Access-Control-* headers\n[18] WithAuthentication         — calls Authenticator chain; injects UserInfo into context; 401 on failure\n[19] WithTracing (optional)     — creates OpenTelemetry span from authenticated context\n[20] WithAudit                  — emits audit events at RequestReceived and ResponseComplete stages\n[21] WithImpersonation          — applies impersonation headers (if Impersonate-* headers are present)\n[22] WithPriorityAndFairness /  — classifies request into a FlowSchema/PriorityLevel; queues or rejects\n    WithMaxInFlightLimit\n[23] WithAuthorization          — calls Authorizer chain; 403 on denial\n[24] (API handler / REST dispatch)\n      │\n      ▼\n    Director → GoRestfulContainer → APIInstaller route\n                                        │\n                                        ▼\n                                  Verb handler (e.g. createHandler)\n                                        │\n                                        ├── Deserialize request body\n                                        ├── Default object fields\n                                        ├── Admission: Mutate (Admit)\n                                        ├── Validate (Strategy + admission Validate)\n                                        └── genericregistry.Store.Create/Update/Delete\n                                                │\n                                                ▼\n                                          DryRunnableStorage\n                                                │\n                                                ▼\n                                          Cacher / etcd3\n```\n\n### 3.2 A Concrete CREATE Example\n\n1. **Request arrives**: `POST /apis/apps/v1/namespaces/default/deployments`\n2. `WithRequestInfo` parses this as `{verb:create, resource:deployments, group:apps, version:v1, namespace:default}`.\n3. `WithAuthentication` validates the bearer token against the OIDC/x509 authenticator chain. UserInfo is injected.\n4. `WithPriorityAndFairness` matches the request to a `FlowSchema` (e.g. `system:authenticated`), places it in a queue for the `workload-high` priority level. The `WorkEstimator` assigns 1 seat (simple mutating write).\n5. `WithAuthorization` calls RBAC — checks if the user has `create` on `deployments` in `default` namespace.\n6. `WithAudit` emits a `RequestReceived` event.\n7. The go-restful `Director` identifies the path is registered under `/apis/apps/v1` and dispatches to the registered go-restful route.\n8. `createHandler` (`handlers/create.go:53`) is invoked:\n   - Content negotiation for request and response encodings.\n   - `limitedReadBodyWithRecordMetric` reads the body (up to `MaxRequestBodyBytes`).\n   - Decoder deserializes body into internal type (`apps/v1` → internal `apps`).\n   - Defaulting functions set optional fields.\n   - Admission `Admit` phase: mutating webhook plugins (and `MutatingAdmissionPolicy`) are called.\n   - Admission `Validate` phase: validating admission plugins (Strategy + `ValidatingAdmissionWebhook` + `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy`) are called.\n   - `genericregistry.Store.Create` is invoked → `DryRunnableStorage.Create` → `Cacher.Create` → `etcd3.Create`.\n   - `WithAudit` emits `ResponseComplete` with the response object (if audit level ≥ `RequestResponse`).\n9. HTTP 201 Created is written back to the client.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Server Composition and Delegation Chain\n\n`kube-apiserver` is not a single server; it is a chain of three `GenericAPIServer` instances assembled in `cmd/kube-apiserver/app/server.go:CreateServerChain`.\n\n```\nIncoming request\n      │\n      ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  Aggregator Server          │  (kube-aggregator)\n│  Handles: APIService CRD    │\n│  Proxies: extension servers │\n└──────────┬──────────────────┘\n           │ delegate (not an APIService path)\n           ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  Kube API Server (core)     │\n│  Handles: /api/v1, /apis/*  │\n│  built-in groups             │\n└──────────┬──────────────────┘\n           │ delegate (unknown path)\n           ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  API Extensions Server      │  (apiextensions-apiserver)\n│  Handles: CRDs dynamically  │\n└──────────┬──────────────────┘\n           │ delegate (end of chain)\n           ▼\n      emptyDelegate → 404\n```\n\n**How delegation works**: Each `GenericAPIServer` stores a `delegationTarget DelegationTarget`. When the `Director`'s `NonGoRestfulMux` cannot match a path, it calls the `UnprotectedHandler()` of the delegation target — effectively bypassing the *target's* filter chain (authentication, authorization, etc. run only once, in the outermost server). This is why `UnprotectedHandler()` returns the target's `Handler.Director` directly, not its `FullHandlerChain`.\n\n**Post-start and pre-shutdown hooks** are aggregated across the chain: each server's hooks are merged with its delegation target's hooks so that a single `RunWithContext` drives the entire system.\n\n---\n\n## 5. API Group Registration\n\n### 5.1 The `runtime.Scheme`\n\nThe `runtime.Scheme` is the type registry for a group. It provides:\n- **GVK ↔ Go type mapping**: registers types so the serialization framework can create the right Go struct from a JSON/protobuf payload.\n- **Conversion functions**: the hub-and-spoke model converts all versions through a single internal type (the \"hub\"). `conversion-gen` generates most conversion functions; handwritten conversions handle non-trivial field renames.\n- **Defaulting functions**: `defaulter-gen` generates `SetDefaults_*` functions that are registered into the scheme and called during decode.\n- **Deep-copy functions**: `deepcopy-gen` generates `DeepCopyObject()` required by `runtime.Object`. This is safety-critical: objects returned from the watch cache must be deep-copied before modification to prevent data races.\n\n### 5.2 `APIGroupInfo` and Registration\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:72\ntype APIGroupInfo struct {\n    PrioritizedVersions         []schema.GroupVersion\n    VersionedResourcesStorageMap map[string]map[string]rest.Storage\n    Scheme                      *runtime.Scheme\n    NegotiatedSerializer        runtime.NegotiatedSerializer\n    ParameterCodec              runtime.ParameterCodec\n}\n```\n\n`VersionedResourcesStorageMap` maps `version → resource-name → rest.Storage`. Each resource can have multiple sub-resources (e.g., `\"deployments/status\"`, `\"deployments/scale\"`), each with its own `rest.Storage` implementation.\n\n`GenericAPIServer.InstallAPIGroups` calls `APIInstaller.Install()` which:\n1. Iterates resources in `VersionedResourcesStorageMap`.\n2. For each resource, introspects which `rest.*` interfaces its `Storage` implements (`rest.Getter`, `rest.Lister`, `rest.Creater`, `rest.Updater`, `rest.GracefulDeleter`, `rest.Watcher`, etc.) via type assertion.\n3. Registers go-restful routes for only the verbs supported by that storage.\n4. Populates discovery documents and OpenAPI schema at the same time.\n\n### 5.3 The `Strategy` Pattern\n\nEach resource's `genericregistry.Store` is configured with up to three strategy objects:\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/registry/generic/registry/store.go:172\nCreateStrategy rest.RESTCreateStrategy\nUpdateStrategy rest.RESTUpdateStrategy\nDeleteStrategy rest.RESTDeleteStrategy\n```\n\nA strategy object implements the resource-specific validation and business logic that is shared across all storage backends. For example, `Deployment`'s strategy validates pod template specs, resets fields that users may not set (like `status`), and enforces name generation rules. This separation keeps the generic storage machinery clean and resource-specific logic co-located with its type definition.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Storage Layer and Watch Cache\n\n### 6.1 Architecture\n\n```\nREST handler\n    │\n    ▼\ngenericregistry.Store\n    │  (wraps in DryRunnableStorage for dry-run)\n    ▼\nCacher  (staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/storage/cacher/cacher.go:262)\n    │   ┌──────────────────────────┐\n    │   │  watchCache              │  ← ring buffer of recent events\n    │   │  cache.Reflector         │  ← LIST + WATCH from etcd\n    │   └──────────────────────────┘\n    │  (falls through for consistent reads or cache misses)\n    ▼\netcd3.Store (grpc to etcd cluster)\n```\n\n### 6.2 Cacher Internals\n\nThe `Cacher` struct (`cacher.go:262`) maintains:\n- `watchCache`: a fixed-capacity ring buffer of `watchCacheEvent` objects, protected by an `RWMutex`. The buffer size defaults to 100 (`EtcdOptions.DefaultWatchCacheSize`), configurable per-resource via `--watch-cache-sizes`.\n- `reflector`: a `cache.Reflector` that issues a single LIST + ongoing WATCH against etcd for the resource prefix. Events are pushed into `incoming chan watchCacheEvent` and then dispatched by the cacher's main goroutine to registered `cacheWatcher` clients.\n- `watchers`: an `indexedWatchers` map that organizes active client watches by namespace and by an optional indexed trigger (e.g., for pod watches filtered by `spec.nodeName`). The trigger optimization avoids waking every watcher on every event.\n\n### 6.3 Serving Semantics\n\n| Request type | Served from |\n|---|---|\n| `?watch=true&resourceVersion=0` | In-memory ring buffer from the start |\n| `?watch=true&resourceVersion=<rv>` | In-memory buffer if rv is within window; otherwise etcd watch |\n| `LIST` (no RV, or `resourceVersion=0`) | Watch cache store snapshot |\n| Consistent `LIST` (`resourceVersionMatch=NotOlderThan` with progress notify) | Waits for cache to advance past etcd's latest rev; served from cache |\n| `LIST` with exact `resourceVersion` and a limit | Falls through to etcd (pagination not fully cached) |\n\nBookmarks (empty `WATCH` events carrying only a `resourceVersion`) prevent the cache's RV from becoming stale — a stale cache would force expensive relist operations on clients.\n\n### 6.4 Optimistic Concurrency\n\nEvery object stored in etcd carries a `resourceVersion` that maps 1-to-1 to etcd's global `mod_revision`. On `UPDATE` or `PATCH`, `GuaranteedUpdate` performs a `txn` in etcd that only commits if the key's `mod_revision` matches the expected value. A mismatch returns `409 Conflict`, requiring the client to re-read and resubmit. This is pure optimistic locking — no server-side distributed locks are held during request processing.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Extension Points and Customization Hooks\n\n### 7.1 Custom Handler Chain (`BuildHandlerChainFunc`)\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/config.go:186\nBuildHandlerChainFunc func(apiHandler http.Handler, c *Config) (secure http.Handler)\n```\n\nBy replacing `Config.BuildHandlerChainFunc`, a consumer can insert, remove, or reorder any filter in the chain. The library ships two implementations:\n- `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` — the standard production chain.\n- `BuildHandlerChainWithStorageVersionPrecondition` — adds `WithStorageVersionPrecondition` (used by `kube-apiserver`) to gate requests until storage version migration is complete.\n\n**Risk**: Replacing the handler chain can silently disable authentication or authorization. Any custom chain should call `DefaultBuildHandlerChain` and wrap its result, rather than starting from scratch.\n\n### 7.2 Post-Start and Pre-Shutdown Hooks\n\n```go\n// GenericAPIServer method\nAddPostStartHook(name string, hook PostStartHookFunc) error\nAddPreShutdownHook(name string, hook preShutdownHookFunc) error\n```\n\nPost-start hooks run after the server starts listening, in separate goroutines. They receive a `StopCh` channel that closes when shutdown is initiated. Pre-shutdown hooks run before the HTTP server stops accepting connections — they are appropriate for draining outbound connections or flipping health check states.\n\n**Risk**: Slow or blocking post-start hooks delay the server's `HasBeenReady` signal, causing the process to be killed by liveness probes in managed environments.\n\n### 7.3 Admission Plugins\n\nAdmission plugins are the primary policy enforcement extension point. A custom in-process plugin must:\n\n1. Implement `admission.Interface` (and optionally `MutationInterface` or `ValidationInterface`).\n2. Register itself: `plugins.Register(PluginName, factory)` — where `plugins` is the `Plugins` registry passed into `AdmissionOptions`.\n3. If it needs shared resources (e.g., an informer factory or a Kubernetes client), implement the appropriate `WantsX` interface from `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/admission/initializer/interfaces.go`:\n   - `WantsExternalKubeInformerFactory`\n   - `WantsAuthorizer`\n   - `WantsRESTMapper`\n   - `WantsFeatures`\n\nFor external validation without in-process code, `ValidatingAdmissionWebhook` (`plugin/webhook/validating`) and the CEL-based `ValidatingAdmissionPolicy` (`plugin/policy/validating`) ship with the library and require no code changes.\n\n**Risk of re-invocation**: Mutating webhooks may be re-invoked if a sibling webhook set `ShouldReinvoke()`. Plugins must therefore be idempotent — applying the same mutation twice must have the same effect as applying it once.\n\n### 7.4 Authenticator and Authorizer Composition\n\nAuthentication is composed via `authenticatorunion` (first-success wins). Authorizers are composed via a chain where each authorizer can return Allow, Deny, or NoOpinion; NoOpinion passes to the next. Adding a custom authenticator or authorizer requires modifying `Config.Authentication.Authenticator` or `Config.Authorization.Authorizer` before `New()` is called.\n\nFor extension servers, `DelegatingAuthenticationOptions` and `DelegatingAuthorizationOptions` (in `pkg/server/options/`) issue `SubjectAccessReview` requests to the main `kube-apiserver`, delegating all auth decisions — this is the standard pattern for aggregated API servers.\n\n### 7.5 `RecommendedOptions` for Extension Servers\n\n```go\n// staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/options/recommended.go:34\ntype RecommendedOptions struct {\n    Etcd           *EtcdOptions\n    SecureServing  *SecureServingOptionsWithLoopback\n    Authentication *DelegatingAuthenticationOptions\n    Authorization  *DelegatingAuthorizationOptions\n    Audit          *AuditOptions\n    Features       *FeatureOptions\n    CoreAPI        *CoreAPIOptions\n    Admission      *AdmissionOptions\n    EgressSelector *EgressSelectorOptions\n    Traces         *TracingOptions\n}\n```\n\nThis struct is the standard entry point for building a new aggregated API server. Calling `ApplyTo(config)` applies each option group to the server config in dependency order (etcd → egress → TLS → auth → audit → admission). Extension server authors only need to set the fields that differ from defaults.\n\n### 7.6 `genericregistry.Store` Hooks\n\nThe `Store` exposes three sets of lifecycle hooks per operation:\n\n| Hook | Timing | Use case |\n|------|--------|----------|\n| `BeginCreate` / `BeginUpdate` | Before storage write; returns commit/revert | Acquire side-effect resources transactionally |\n| `AfterCreate` / `AfterUpdate` / `AfterDelete` | After storage write, before decoration | Emit metrics, invalidate caches |\n| `Decorator` | On every object returned from storage | Inject computed fields not stored in etcd |\n| `ShouldDeleteDuringUpdate` | During update | Implement finalizer-style soft-delete |\n\n**Risk**: `BeginCreate/BeginUpdate` revert functions must be idempotent because they may be called more than once if the framework retries internally.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 8.1 etcd Dependency\n\nThe `apiserver` library has a hard dependency on a live etcd cluster. There is no in-memory fallback for production use. A full etcd partition causes write operations to fail immediately (etcd requires a quorum write). Read operations that hit the watch cache can continue to succeed for cached data, but consistent reads (`resourceVersionMatch=NotOlderThan`) will block until `waitUntilFreshAndBlock` times out.\n\n### 8.2 Watch Cache Eviction\n\nThe watch cache ring buffer is bounded. When a slow client falls behind the write rate, its cached RV drops out of the buffer window and the watcher receives an `ERROR` event with `410 Gone`. The client must restart its watch from `resourceVersion=0`. This is a known operational cliff: high write rates or large ring buffer sizes (configured per-resource) directly trade memory for client resilience. The default buffer size is 100 events per resource type.\n\n### 8.3 Priority and Fairness vs. MaxInFlight\n\nThe library supports two concurrency-control mechanisms:\n- **`MaxInFlightLimit`** (`pkg/server/filters/maxinflight.go`): a simple counting semaphore with separate limits for mutating (`MaxMutatingRequestsInFlight`) and non-mutating (`MaxRequestsInFlight`) requests. Any request exceeding the limit gets an immediate `429 Too Many Requests`.\n- **Priority and Fairness (APF)**: replaces `MaxInFlightLimit` when `Config.FlowControl` is non-nil. APF queues requests rather than immediately rejecting them, and enforces per-flow fairness using shuffle-sharded fair queuing.\n\nAPF is the default in `kube-apiserver` since 1.29. Extension servers using `RecommendedOptions` do not enable APF by default and fall back to `MaxInFlightLimit`. APF requires `FlowSchema` and `PriorityLevelConfiguration` objects in the API to exist before it can configure itself — creating a startup bootstrap dependency.\n\n### 8.4 Admission Re-invocation Overhead\n\nThe `reinvoker` wrapper (`pkg/admission/reinvocation.go`) can run the mutating admission chain twice per write request. With many heavy mutating webhooks, this doubles the worst-case webhook call latency. Operators should use `reinvocationPolicy: IfNeeded` selectively on `MutatingWebhookConfiguration` objects rather than globally.\n\n### 8.5 Handler Chain Construction Ordering\n\n`DefaultBuildHandlerChain` is built inside-out (code reads bottom-to-top). Misplacing a filter — e.g., moving `WithRequestInfo` after `WithAuthentication` — silently breaks features that depend on `RequestInfo` being in context (such as RBAC authorization, which inspects resource/verb). The framework does not validate the chain ordering at startup; bugs manifest at runtime under specific request patterns.\n\n### 8.6 Graceful Shutdown Sequencing\n\nThe shutdown flow (`RunWithContext`, `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/genericapiserver.go:536`) relies on a precise signal ordering:\n\n```\nSIGTERM/ctx.Done()\n    → ShutdownInitiated (readyz → red)\n    → PreShutdownHooks run concurrently\n    → AfterShutdownDelayDuration (~70s default)\n    → NotAcceptingNewRequest\n        → NonLongRunningRequestWaitGroup.Wait (up to ShutdownTimeout ~60s)\n        → WatchRequestWaitGroup.Wait\n    → InFlightRequestsDrained\n        → HTTP server stops listening\n        → AuditBackend.Shutdown()\n```\n\nA pre-shutdown hook that blocks longer than `ShutdownDelayDuration` can prevent the server from draining active requests cleanly. `WithWaitGroup` ensures that each non-long-running request adds itself to `NonLongRunningRequestWaitGroup` only if it is not exempt from `RetryAfter` logic, so these two mechanisms must agree on what \"long-running\" means.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Operational Implications\n\n### 9.1 Performance: Watch Cache Sizing\n\nEach watched resource type maintains its own ring buffer. Resources with high write throughput (e.g., `Events`, `Pods` in large clusters) should have their cache size tuned via `--watch-cache-sizes=pods#1000,events#0`. Setting a resource's cache to 0 disables caching for that type and pushes all watch traffic directly to etcd — useful for resources that are rarely watched but have large objects.\n\nThe metric `watch_cache_resource_version` (label: `resource`) tracks the cache's current RV. A value significantly behind etcd's compaction watermark indicates the cache is not keeping up and clients will experience frequent relist events.\n\n### 9.2 Performance: Admission Latency\n\nEvery mutating or creating request passes through the full admission chain synchronously. Slow webhooks directly increase P99 latency for all writes. The filter `WithAudit` annotates audit events with per-stage timestamps; the metric `apiserver_admission_webhook_request_total{name, operation, type, rejected}` and `apiserver_admission_webhook_latencies_seconds` track per-webhook performance.\n\nTo isolate the impact: a single slow webhook adds its full RTT to every matching request. Webhook timeout defaults are 10s; a webhook timing out results in a 500 (fail-open) or a rejection (fail-closed), depending on `failurePolicy`.\n\n### 9.3 Reliability: Health Endpoints\n\nThe server exposes three health gates via `NonGoRestfulMux`:\n- `/healthz` — liveness. Fails only on catastrophic internal state. Added checks via `AddHealthChecks`.\n- `/readyz` — readiness. Fails during startup and the shutdown delay period (as soon as `ShutdownInitiated` fires). Custom storage readiness checks can be added via `StorageReadinessHook`.\n- `/livez` — liveness with a grace period (`livezGracePeriod`) that allows the server to start up without being killed by kubelet.\n\n`MuxAndDiscoveryComplete` is a lifecycle signal used by the `WithMuxAndDiscoveryComplete` filter to return `503` for requests that arrive before all paths are registered. This prevents 404 responses from being cached by clients during server startup.\n\n### 9.4 Maintainability: Code Generation Dependencies\n\nThe `apiserver` library's build correctness depends on running several code generators before compilation:\n- `deepcopy-gen`: for `DeepCopyObject()` on all registered types.\n- `conversion-gen`: for conversion functions between internal and external API versions.\n- `defaulter-gen`: for `SetDefaults_*` functions.\n- `openapi-gen`: for `GetOpenAPIDefinitions()` used to build the `/openapi/v2` and `/openapi/v3` specs.\n\nForgetting to regenerate code after adding a new field is a common source of subtle bugs — the field will be silently dropped during conversion if no conversion function copies it, and will be missing from OpenAPI if not annotated.\n\n### 9.5 Scalability: Resource Version Monotonicity\n\nAll `resourceVersion` values are etcd `mod_revision` values — globally monotonically increasing across all resources. This simplifies consistency reasoning (there is a total ordering of all writes) but means the RV of a `ConfigMap` and a `Pod` are comparable in magnitude. Clients that filter watches by a very high RV may cause the cacher's `waitUntilFreshAndBlock` to wait indefinitely if the RV is ahead of the cache — exposing a denial-of-service surface. The `WatchCacheReadWait` histogram tracks time spent in this blocking path.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Important Files and Directories\n\n```\nstaging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/\n├── ARCHITECTURE.md                          # High-level design overview (canonical)\n├── pkg/\n│   ├── server/\n│   │   ├── config.go                        #
